					<h1 id="firstHeading" class="firstHeading mw-first-heading"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Ecuador</span></h1>
							
<div id="p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown mw-portlet mw-portlet-lang"  >
	<input type="checkbox" id="p-lang-btn-checkbox" role="button" aria-haspopup="true" data-event-name="ui.dropdown-p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown-checkbox mw-interlanguage-selector" aria-label="Go to an article in another language. Available in 245 languages"   >
	<label id="p-lang-btn-label" for="p-lang-btn-checkbox" class="vector-dropdown-label cdx-button cdx-button--fake-button cdx-button--fake-button--enabled cdx-button--weight-quiet cdx-button--action-progressive mw-portlet-lang-heading-245" aria-hidden="true"  ><span class="vector-icon mw-ui-icon-language-progressive mw-ui-icon-wikimedia-language-progressive"></span>

<span class="vector-dropdown-label-text">245 languages</span>
	</label>
	<div class="vector-dropdown-content">

		<div class="vector-menu-content">
			
			<ul class="vector-menu-content-list">

class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/.ec" title=".ec">.ec</a></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><div class="ib-country-fn"><ol class="ib-country-fn-alpha">
<li value="1">Including <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands" title="Galápagos Islands">Galápagos Islands</a>.</li><li value="2"><a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_sucre" title="Ecuadorian sucre">Sucre</a> until 2000, replaced by the US$ and <a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_centavo_coins" title="Ecuadorian centavo coins">Ecuadorian centavo coins</a>.</li>
</ol></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Ecuador</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/ɛ/: &#39;e&#39; in &#39;dress&#39;">ɛ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="&#39;w&#39; in &#39;wind&#39;">w</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span><span title="/ɔːr/: &#39;ar&#39; in &#39;war&#39;">ɔːr</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/8\/89\/En-us-Ecuador.ogg\/En-us-Ecuador.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;En-us-Ecuador.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/8/89/En-us-Ecuador.ogg/En-us-Ecuador.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Ecuador.ogg" title="File:En-us-Ecuador.ogg">i</a></sup></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">EK</span>-wə-dor</i></a>; <small>Spanish pronunciation:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="es-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Spanish" title="Help:IPA/Spanish">[ekwaˈðoɾ]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-2" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/f\/fb\/ES-pe_-_Ecuador.ogg\/ES-pe_-_Ecuador.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;ES-pe - Ecuador.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/f/fb/ES-pe_-_Ecuador.ogg/ES-pe_-_Ecuador.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:ES-pe_-_Ecuador.ogg" title="File:ES-pe - Ecuador.ogg">i</a></sup>; <a href="/wiki/Quechuan_languages" title="Quechuan languages">Quechua</a>: <i>Ikwayur</i>; <a href="/wiki/Shuar_language" title="Shuar language">Shuar</a>: <i>Ecuador</i> or <i>Ekuatur</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> officially the <b>Republic of Ecuador</b> (<a href="/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language">Spanish</a>: <i lang="es">República del Ecuador</i>,  which literally translates as "Republic of the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">Equator</a>"; <a href="/wiki/Quechuan_languages" title="Quechuan languages">Quechua</a>: <i>Ikwayur Ripuwlika</i>; <a href="/wiki/Shuar_language" title="Shuar language">Shuar</a>: <i>Ekuatur Nunka</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> is a country in northwestern <a href="/wiki/South_America" title="South America">South America</a>, bordered by <a href="/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia">Colombia</a> on the north, <a href="/wiki/Peru" title="Peru">Peru</a> on the east and south, and the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a> on the west. Ecuador also includes the <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands" title="Galápagos Islands">Galápagos Islands</a> in the Pacific, about 1,000 kilometers (621&#160;mi) west of the mainland. The country's <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> and largest city is <a href="/wiki/Quito" title="Quito">Quito</a>.
</p><p>The territories of modern-day Ecuador were once home to a variety of <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_in_Ecuador" title="Indigenous peoples in Ecuador">Indigenous</a> groups that were gradually incorporated into the <a href="/wiki/Inca_Empire" title="Inca Empire">Inca Empire</a> during the 15th century. The territory was <a href="/wiki/Spanish_colonization_of_the_Americas" title="Spanish colonization of the Americas">colonized by Spain</a> during the 16th century, achieving independence in 1820 as part of <a href="/wiki/Gran_Colombia" title="Gran Colombia">Gran Colombia</a>, from which it emerged as its own sovereign state in 1830. The legacy of both empires is reflected in Ecuador's ethnically diverse population, with most of its 17.8 million people being <a href="/wiki/Mestizo" title="Mestizo">mestizos</a>, followed by large minorities of <a href="/wiki/Europeans" class="mw-redirect" title="Europeans">Europeans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_in_Ecuador" title="Indigenous peoples in Ecuador">Native American</a>, <a href="/wiki/Afro-Ecuadorians" title="Afro-Ecuadorians">African</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Asian_people" title="Asian people">Asian</a> descendants. Spanish is the official language and is spoken by a majority of the population, though 13 Native languages are also recognized, including <a href="/wiki/Quechuan_languages" title="Quechuan languages">Quechua</a> and <a href="/wiki/Shuar_language" title="Shuar language">Shuar</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_state" title="Sovereign state">sovereign state</a> of Ecuador is a <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> republic and a <a href="/wiki/Developing_country" title="Developing country">developing country</a><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> whose economy is highly dependent on exports of commodities, namely petroleum and agricultural products. It is governed as a democratic <a href="/wiki/Presidential_system" title="Presidential system">presidential</a> republic. The country is a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/Organization_of_American_States" title="Organization of American States">Organization of American States</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mercosur" title="Mercosur">Mercosur</a>, <a href="/wiki/Forum_for_the_Progress_and_Development_of_South_America" class="mw-redirect" title="Forum for the Progress and Development of South America">PROSUR</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/Center_for_Economic_and_Policy_Research" title="Center for Economic and Policy Research">Center for Economic and Policy Research</a>, between 2006 and 2016, poverty decreased from 36.7% to 22.5% and annual per capita GDP growth was 1.5 percent (as compared to 0.6 percent over the prior two decades). At the same time, the country's <a href="/wiki/Gini_coefficient" title="Gini coefficient">Gini index</a> of economic inequality decreased from 0.55 to 0.47.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>One of 17 <a href="/wiki/Megadiverse_countries" title="Megadiverse countries">megadiverse countries</a> in the world,<sup id="cite_ref-Conservation_International_Site_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Conservation_International_Site-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Ecuador hosts many <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic</a> plants and animals, such as those of the <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands" title="Galápagos Islands">Galápagos Islands</a>. In recognition of its unique ecological heritage, the new constitution of 2008 is the first in the world to recognize legally enforceable <a href="/wiki/Rights_of_Nature" class="mw-redirect" title="Rights of Nature">Rights of Nature</a>, or ecosystem rights.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The country's name means "<a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">Equator</a>" in Spanish, truncated from the Spanish official name, <i>República del Ecuador</i> (<abbr title="literally">lit.</abbr> "Republic of the Equator"), derived from the former <a href="/wiki/Ecuador_Department" title="Ecuador Department">Ecuador Department</a> of <a href="/wiki/Gran_Colombia" title="Gran Colombia">Gran Colombia</a> established in 1824 as a division of the former territory of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Audience_of_Quito" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Audience of Quito">Royal Audience of Quito</a>. Quito, which remained the capital of the department and republic, is located only about 40 kilometers (25&#160;mi), <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1154941027">.mw-parser-output .frac{white-space:nowrap}.mw-parser-output .frac .num,.mw-parser-output .frac .den{font-size:80%;line-height:0;vertical-align:super}.mw-parser-output .frac .den{vertical-align:sub}.mw-parser-output .sr-only{border:0;clip:rect(0,0,0,0);clip-path:polygon(0px 0px,0px 0px,0px 0px);height:1px;margin:-1px;overflow:hidden;padding:0;position:absolute;width:1px}</style><span class="frac" role="math"><span class="num">1</span>&#8260;<span class="den">4</span></span> of a <a href="/wiki/Latitude#Subdivisions" title="Latitude">degree</a>, south of the equator.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Ecuador" title="History of Ecuador">History of Ecuador</a> and <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_in_Ecuador" title="Indigenous peoples in Ecuador">Indigenous peoples in Ecuador</a></div>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pre-Inca_era">Pre-Inca era</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Pre-Inca era">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tolita_mythological_figure_in_feathered_costume_IMJ_B77.0161.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Tolita_mythological_figure_in_feathered_costume_IMJ_B77.0161.jpg/170px-Tolita_mythological_figure_in_feathered_costume_IMJ_B77.0161.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="254" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Tolita_mythological_figure_in_feathered_costume_IMJ_B77.0161.jpg/255px-Tolita_mythological_figure_in_feathered_costume_IMJ_B77.0161.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Tolita_mythological_figure_in_feathered_costume_IMJ_B77.0161.jpg/340px-Tolita_mythological_figure_in_feathered_costume_IMJ_B77.0161.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2591" data-file-height="3870" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/History_of_Ecuador#La_Tolita_culture" title="History of Ecuador">Tumaco-La Tolita</a> mythological figure in feathered costume. Between 100 BC and 100 AD. Found in <a href="/wiki/Esmeraldas,_Ecuador" title="Esmeraldas, Ecuador">Esmeraldas</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Various peoples had settled in the area of future Ecuador before the arrival of the <a href="/wiki/Incas" class="mw-redirect" title="Incas">Incas</a>. The archeological evidence suggests that the <a href="/wiki/Paleo-Indians" title="Paleo-Indians">Paleo-Indians</a>' first dispersal into the Americas occurred near the end of the <a href="/wiki/Last_glacial_period" class="mw-redirect" title="Last glacial period">last glacial period</a>, around 16,500–13,000 years ago. The first people who reached Ecuador may have journeyed by land from <a href="/wiki/North_America" title="North America">North</a> and <a href="/wiki/Central_America" title="Central America">Central America</a> or by boat down the Pacific Ocean coastline.
</p><p>Even though their languages were unrelated, these groups developed similar groups of cultures, each based in different environments. The people of the coast combined agriculture with fishing, hunting, and gathering; the people of the highland Andes developed a sedentary agricultural way of life; and peoples of the Amazon basin relied on hunting and gathering, in some cases combined with agriculture and <a href="/wiki/Arboriculture" title="Arboriculture">arboriculture</a>.
</p><p>Many civilizations<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> arose in Ecuador, such as the <a href="/wiki/Valdivia_Culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Valdivia Culture">Valdivia Culture</a> and <a href="/wiki/Machalilla_Culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Machalilla Culture">Machalilla Culture</a> on the coast,<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/Quitu_culture" title="Quitu culture">Quitus</a> (near present-day Quito),<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/Ca%C3%B1ari" title="Cañari">Cañari</a> (near present-day <a href="/wiki/Cuenca,_Ecuador" title="Cuenca, Ecuador">Cuenca</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>   Each civilization developed its own distinctive architecture, pottery, and religious interests.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>
In the highland Andes mountains, where life was more sedentary, groups of tribes cooperated and formed villages; thus the first nations based on agricultural resources and the domestication of animals formed. Eventually, through wars and marriage alliances of their <a href="/wiki/Leader" class="mw-redirect" title="Leader">leaders</a>, groups of nations formed confederations.</p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ecuador_ingapirca_inca_ruins.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Ecuador_ingapirca_inca_ruins.jpg/220px-Ecuador_ingapirca_inca_ruins.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Ecuador_ingapirca_inca_ruins.jpg/330px-Ecuador_ingapirca_inca_ruins.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Ecuador_ingapirca_inca_ruins.jpg/440px-Ecuador_ingapirca_inca_ruins.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1720" data-file-height="1148" /></a><figcaption>Ruins of <a href="/wiki/Ingapirca" title="Ingapirca">Ingapirca</a>, this site served as an outpost and provisioning of the Incan troops, but mainly it was a place of worship and veneration to the sun, the supreme Inca God, thus constituting a <a href="/wiki/Coricancha" title="Coricancha">Coricancha</a>, dedicated to the Inca ritual.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:M%C3%BAmia_de_cabe%C3%A7a_-_Povo_Jivaro_01.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/M%C3%BAmia_de_cabe%C3%A7a_-_Povo_Jivaro_01.jpg/170px-M%C3%BAmia_de_cabe%C3%A7a_-_Povo_Jivaro_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="173" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/M%C3%BAmia_de_cabe%C3%A7a_-_Povo_Jivaro_01.jpg/255px-M%C3%BAmia_de_cabe%C3%A7a_-_Povo_Jivaro_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/M%C3%BAmia_de_cabe%C3%A7a_-_Povo_Jivaro_01.jpg/340px-M%C3%BAmia_de_cabe%C3%A7a_-_Povo_Jivaro_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1715" data-file-height="1745" /></a><figcaption>Pre-Columbian shrunken head of the <a href="/wiki/Shuar" title="Shuar">Shuars</a> (<a href="/wiki/Jivaroan_peoples" title="Jivaroan peoples">Jivaroan peoples</a>).</figcaption></figure>
<p>When the <a href="/wiki/Incas" class="mw-redirect" title="Incas">Incas</a> arrived, they found that these confederations were so developed that it took the Incas two generations of rulers—<a href="/wiki/Topa_Inca_Yupanqui" title="Topa Inca Yupanqui">Topa Inca Yupanqui</a> and <a href="/wiki/Huayna_Capac" title="Huayna Capac">Huayna Capac</a>—to absorb them into the <a href="/wiki/Inca_Empire" title="Inca Empire">Inca Empire</a>. People belonging to the confederations that gave them the most problems were deported to distant areas of Peru, Bolivia, and north Argentina. Similarly, a number of loyal Inca subjects from Peru and Bolivia were brought to Ecuador to prevent rebellion. Thus, the region of highland Ecuador became part of the <a href="/wiki/Inca_Empire" title="Inca Empire">Inca Empire</a> in 1463 sharing the same language.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In contrast, when the Incas made incursions into coastal Ecuador and the eastern Amazon jungles of Ecuador, they found both the environment and indigenous people more hostile. Moreover, when the Incas tried to subdue them, these indigenous people withdrew to the interior and resorted to <a href="/wiki/Guerrilla" class="mw-redirect" title="Guerrilla">guerrilla</a> tactics. As a result, Inca expansion into the Amazon Basin and the Pacific coast of Ecuador was hampered. The indigenous people of the Amazon jungle and coastal Ecuador remained relatively autonomous until the Spanish soldiers and missionaries arrived in force. The Amazonian people and the <a href="/wiki/Chachi_people" title="Chachi people">Cayapas</a> of Coastal Ecuador were the only groups to resist both Inca and Spanish domination, maintaining their languages and cultures well into the 21st century.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Before the arrival of the Spaniards, the Inca Empire was involved in a <a href="/wiki/Inca_Civil_War" title="Inca Civil War">civil war</a>. The untimely death of both the heir <a href="/wiki/Ninan_Cuyochi" title="Ninan Cuyochi">Ninan Cuyochi</a> and the Emperor Huayna Capac, from a European disease that spread into Ecuador, created a power vacuum between two factions and led to a civil war.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The army stationed north<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> headed by Atahualpa marched south to Cuzco and massacred the royal family associated with his brother. In 1532, a small band of Spaniards headed by Francisco Pizarro reached <a href="/wiki/Cajamarca" title="Cajamarca">Cajamarca</a> and lured Atahualpa into a trap (<a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Cajamarca" title="Battle of Cajamarca">battle of Cajamarca</a>). <a href="/wiki/Pizarro" class="mw-redirect" title="Pizarro">Pizarro</a> promised to release Atahualpa if he made good his promise of filling a room full of gold. But, after a mock trial, the Spaniards executed Atahualpa by strangulation.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Spanish_colonization">Spanish colonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Spanish colonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Plaza_principal_de_Quito,_an%C3%B3nimo_-_siglo_XVIII_(Museo_de_la_Moneda,_Bogot%C3%A1).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Plaza_principal_de_Quito%2C_an%C3%B3nimo_-_siglo_XVIII_%28Museo_de_la_Moneda%2C_Bogot%C3%A1%29.jpg/220px-Plaza_principal_de_Quito%2C_an%C3%B3nimo_-_siglo_XVIII_%28Museo_de_la_Moneda%2C_Bogot%C3%A1%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Plaza_principal_de_Quito%2C_an%C3%B3nimo_-_siglo_XVIII_%28Museo_de_la_Moneda%2C_Bogot%C3%A1%29.jpg/330px-Plaza_principal_de_Quito%2C_an%C3%B3nimo_-_siglo_XVIII_%28Museo_de_la_Moneda%2C_Bogot%C3%A1%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Plaza_principal_de_Quito%2C_an%C3%B3nimo_-_siglo_XVIII_%28Museo_de_la_Moneda%2C_Bogot%C3%A1%29.jpg/440px-Plaza_principal_de_Quito%2C_an%C3%B3nimo_-_siglo_XVIII_%28Museo_de_la_Moneda%2C_Bogot%C3%A1%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="926" data-file-height="686" /></a><figcaption>Major square of Quito. Painting of 18th century. <a href="/wiki/Quito_School" title="Quito School">Quito Painting Colonial School</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>New infectious diseases such as <a href="/wiki/Smallpox" title="Smallpox">smallpox</a>, endemic to the Europeans, caused high fatalities among the Amerindian population during the first decades of Spanish rule, as they had no <a href="/wiki/Immunity_(medical)" title="Immunity (medical)">immunity</a>. At the same time, the natives were forced into the <i><a href="/wiki/Encomienda" title="Encomienda">encomienda</a></i> labor system for the Spanish. In 1563, <a href="/wiki/Quito" title="Quito">Quito</a> became the seat of a <a href="/wiki/Royal_Audience_of_Quito" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Audience of Quito">real audiencia</a> (administrative district) of Spain and part of the <a href="/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_Peru" title="Viceroyalty of Peru">Viceroyalty of Peru</a> and later the <a href="/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Granada" title="Viceroyalty of New Granada">Viceroyalty of New Granada</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/1797_Riobamba_earthquake" title="1797 Riobamba earthquake">1797 Riobamba earthquake</a>, which caused up to 40,000 casualties, was studied by <a href="/wiki/Alexander_von_Humboldt" title="Alexander von Humboldt">Alexander von Humboldt</a>, when he visited the area in 1801–1802.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After nearly 300 years of Spanish rule, Quito still remained small with a population of 10,000 people. On 10 August 1809, the city's <i><a href="/wiki/Criollo_people" title="Criollo people">criollos</a></i> called for independence from Spain (first among the peoples of Latin America). They were led by Juan Pío Montúfar, Quiroga, Salinas, and Bishop Cuero y Caicedo. Quito's nickname, "<i><a href="/wiki/Luz_de_Am%C3%A9rica" class="mw-redirect" title="Luz de América">Luz de América</a></i>" ("Light of America"), is based on its leading role in trying to secure an independent, local government. Although the new government lasted no more than two months, it had important repercussions and was an inspiration for the independence movement of the rest of Spanish America. Today, 10 August is celebrated as Independence Day, a <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Ecuador" title="Public holidays in Ecuador">national holiday</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence">Independence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Independence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_War_of_Independence" title="Ecuadorian War of Independence">Ecuadorian War of Independence</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Jose_Antonio_de_Sucre.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Jose_Antonio_de_Sucre.JPG/170px-Jose_Antonio_de_Sucre.JPG" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Jose_Antonio_de_Sucre.JPG/255px-Jose_Antonio_de_Sucre.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Jose_Antonio_de_Sucre.JPG/340px-Jose_Antonio_de_Sucre.JPG 2x" data-file-width="460" data-file-height="614" /></a><figcaption>Venezuelan independence leader <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Jos%C3%A9_de_Sucre" title="Antonio José de Sucre">Antonio José de Sucre</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Entrevista_de_Guayaquil.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Entrevista_de_Guayaquil.jpg/170px-Entrevista_de_Guayaquil.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="160" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Entrevista_de_Guayaquil.jpg/255px-Entrevista_de_Guayaquil.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Entrevista_de_Guayaquil.jpg/340px-Entrevista_de_Guayaquil.jpg 2x" data-file-width="577" data-file-height="543" /></a><figcaption>The "<a href="/wiki/Guayaquil_Conference" title="Guayaquil Conference">Guayaquil Conference</a>" was the meeting between the two main Spanish South American independence leaders. In it the form of government of the nascent countries was discussed, <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_San_Mart%C3%ADn" title="José de San Martín">San Martín</a> opted for a unified South America in the form of a monarchy, while <a href="/wiki/Sim%C3%B3n_Bol%C3%ADvar" title="Simón Bolívar">Bolívar</a> opted for the same but into a republic. 1843 painting.</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 9 October 1820, the Department of <a href="/wiki/Guayaquil" title="Guayaquil">Guayaquil</a> became the first territory in Ecuador to gain its independence from Spain, and it spawned most of the Ecuadorian coastal provinces, establishing itself as an independent state. Its inhabitants celebrated what is now Ecuador's official Independence Day on 24 May 1822. The rest of Ecuador gained its independence after <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Jos%C3%A9_de_Sucre" title="Antonio José de Sucre">Antonio José de Sucre</a> defeated the Spanish Royalist forces at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Pichincha" title="Battle of Pichincha">Battle of Pichincha</a>, near <a href="/wiki/Quito" title="Quito">Quito</a>. Following the battle, Ecuador joined <a href="/wiki/Sim%C3%B3n_Bol%C3%ADvar" title="Simón Bolívar">Simón Bolívar</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Gran_Colombia" class="mw-redirect" title="Republic of Gran Colombia">Republic of Gran Colombia</a>, also including modern-day <a href="/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia">Colombia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Panama" title="Panama">Panama</a>. In 1830, Ecuador separated from Gran Colombia and became an independent republic. Two years later, it annexed the <a href="/wiki/Galapagos_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Galapagos Islands">Galapagos Islands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 19th century was marked by instability for Ecuador with a rapid succession of rulers. The first president of Ecuador was the Venezuelan-born <a href="/wiki/Juan_Jos%C3%A9_Flores" title="Juan José Flores">Juan José Flores</a>, who was ultimately deposed. Leaders who followed him included <a href="/wiki/Vicente_Rocafuerte" title="Vicente Rocafuerte">Vicente Rocafuerte</a>; <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Joaqu%C3%ADn_de_Olmedo" title="José Joaquín de Olmedo">José Joaquín de Olmedo</a>; <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mar%C3%ADa_Urbina" class="mw-redirect" title="José María Urbina">José María Urbina</a>; <a href="/wiki/Diego_Noboa" title="Diego Noboa">Diego Noboa</a>; <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Jos%C3%A9_de_Arteta" title="Pedro José de Arteta">Pedro José de Arteta</a>; <a href="/wiki/Manuel_de_Asc%C3%A1subi" title="Manuel de Ascásubi">Manuel de Ascásubi</a>; and Flores's own son, <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Flores_Jij%C3%B3n" title="Antonio Flores Jijón">Antonio Flores Jijón</a>, among others. The conservative <a href="/wiki/Gabriel_Garc%C3%ADa_Moreno" title="Gabriel García Moreno">Gabriel García Moreno</a> unified the country in the 1860s with the support of the Roman Catholic Church. In the late 19th century, world demand for <a href="/wiki/Cocoa_bean" title="Cocoa bean">cocoa</a> tied the economy to commodity exports and led to migrations from the highlands to the agricultural frontier on the coast.
</p><p>Ecuador <a href="/wiki/Abolition_of_slavery_timeline" class="mw-redirect" title="Abolition of slavery timeline">abolished</a> <a href="/wiki/Slavery_in_Latin_America" title="Slavery in Latin America">slavery</a> in 1851.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> The descendants of enslaved Ecuadorians are among today's <a href="/wiki/Afro-Ecuadorian" class="mw-redirect" title="Afro-Ecuadorian">Afro-Ecuadorian</a> population.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Liberal_Revolution">Liberal Revolution</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Liberal Revolution">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Historic_Center_of_Quito_-_World_Heritage_Site_by_UNESCO_-_Photo_437.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Historic_Center_of_Quito_-_World_Heritage_Site_by_UNESCO_-_Photo_437.jpg/220px-Historic_Center_of_Quito_-_World_Heritage_Site_by_UNESCO_-_Photo_437.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Historic_Center_of_Quito_-_World_Heritage_Site_by_UNESCO_-_Photo_437.jpg/330px-Historic_Center_of_Quito_-_World_Heritage_Site_by_UNESCO_-_Photo_437.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Historic_Center_of_Quito_-_World_Heritage_Site_by_UNESCO_-_Photo_437.jpg/440px-Historic_Center_of_Quito_-_World_Heritage_Site_by_UNESCO_-_Photo_437.jpg 2x" data-file-width="506" data-file-height="380" /></a><figcaption>Antique <a href="/wiki/Dugout_(boat)" class="mw-redirect" title="Dugout (boat)">dug out canoes</a> in the courtyard of the Old Military Hospital in the <a href="/wiki/Historic_Center_of_Quito" class="mw-redirect" title="Historic Center of Quito">Historic Center of Quito</a></figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Liberal_Revolution_of_1895" title="Liberal Revolution of 1895">Liberal Revolution of 1895</a></div>
<p>The Liberal Revolution of 1895 under <a href="/wiki/Eloy_Alfaro" title="Eloy Alfaro">Eloy Alfaro</a> reduced the power of the clergy and the conservative land owners. This liberal wing retained power until the military "Julian Revolution" of 1925. The 1930s and 1940s were marked by instability and emergence of populist politicians, such as five-time President <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mar%C3%ADa_Velasco_Ibarra" title="José María Velasco Ibarra">José María Velasco Ibarra</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Loss_of_claimed_territories_since_1830">Loss of claimed territories since 1830</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Loss of claimed territories since 1830">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Ecuadorian%E2%80%93Peruvian_territorial_dispute" class="mw-redirect" title="History of the Ecuadorian–Peruvian territorial dispute">History of the Ecuadorian–Peruvian territorial dispute</a></div>

.navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a
