<p>The provinces are divided into <a href="/wiki/Cantons_of_Ecuador" title="Cantons of Ecuador">cantons</a> and further subdivided into parishes (<i>parroquias</i>).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Regions_and_planning_areas">Regions and planning areas</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Regions and planning areas">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Regionalization, or zoning, is the union of two or more adjoining provinces in order to decentralize the administrative functions of the capital, Quito.
In Ecuador, there are seven regions, or zones, each shaped by the following provinces:
</p>
<ul><li>Region 1 (42,126&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, or 16,265&#160;mi<sup>2</sup>): <a href="/wiki/Esmeraldas_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Esmeraldas (province)">Esmeraldas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carchi" class="mw-redirect" title="Carchi">Carchi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Imbabura_Province" title="Imbabura Province">Imbabura</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sucumbios" class="mw-redirect" title="Sucumbios">Sucumbios</a>. Administrative city: <a href="/wiki/Ibarra,_Ecuador" title="Ibarra, Ecuador">Ibarra</a></li>
<li>Region 2 (43,498&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, or 16,795&#160;mi<sup>2</sup>): <a href="/wiki/Pichincha_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pichincha (province)">Pichincha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Napo_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Napo (province)">Napo</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Orellana_Province" title="Orellana Province">Orellana</a>. Administrative city: <a href="/wiki/Tena,_Ecuador" title="Tena, Ecuador">Tena</a></li>
<li>Region 3 (44,710&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, or 17,263&#160;mi<sup>2</sup>): <a href="/wiki/Chimborazo_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Chimborazo (province)">Chimborazo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tungurahua_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tungurahua (province)">Tungurahua</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pastaza_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pastaza (province)">Pastaza</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Cotopaxi_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cotopaxi (province)">Cotopaxi</a>. Administrative city: <a href="/wiki/Riobamba,_Ecuador" class="mw-redirect" title="Riobamba, Ecuador">Riobamba</a></li>
<li>Region 4 (22,257&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, or 8,594&#160;mi<sup>2</sup>): <a href="/wiki/Manab%C3%AD_Province" title="Manabí Province">Manabí</a> and <a href="/wiki/Santo_Domingo_de_los_Tsachilas" class="mw-redirect" title="Santo Domingo de los Tsachilas">Santo Domingo de los Tsachilas</a>. Administrative city: <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ciudad_Alfaro,_Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ciudad Alfaro, Ecuador (page does not exist)">Ciudad Alfaro</a></li>
<li>Region 5 (38,420&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, or 14,834&#160;mi<sup>2</sup>): <a href="/wiki/Santa_Elena_Province" title="Santa Elena Province">Santa Elena</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guayas_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Guayas (province)">Guayas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Los_R%C3%ADos_Province" title="Los Ríos Province">Los Ríos</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Province" title="Galápagos Province">Galápagos</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Bol%C3%ADvar_Province_(Ecuador)" title="Bolívar Province (Ecuador)">Bolívar</a>. Administrative city: <a href="/wiki/Milagro,_Ecuador" title="Milagro, Ecuador">Milagro</a></li>
<li>Region 6 (38,237&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, or 14,763&#160;mi<sup>2</sup>): <a href="/wiki/Ca%C3%B1ar_Province,_Ecuador" class="mw-redirect" title="Cañar Province, Ecuador">Cañar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Azuay" class="mw-redirect" title="Azuay">Azuay</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Morona_Santiago" class="mw-redirect" title="Morona Santiago">Morona Santiago</a>. Administrative city: <a href="/wiki/Cuenca_(Ecuador)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuenca (Ecuador)">Cuenca</a></li>
<li>Region 7 (27,571&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, or 10,645&#160;mi<sup>2</sup>): <a href="/wiki/El_Oro_Province" title="El Oro Province">El Oro</a>, <a href="/wiki/Loja_Province" title="Loja Province">Loja</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Zamora_Chinchipe" class="mw-redirect" title="Zamora Chinchipe">Zamora Chinchipe</a>. Administrative city: <a href="/wiki/Loja,_Ecuador" title="Loja, Ecuador">Loja</a></li></ul>
<p><a href="/wiki/Quito" title="Quito">Quito</a> and <a href="/wiki/Guayaquil" title="Guayaquil">Guayaquil</a> are Metropolitan Districts. <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos" class="mw-redirect" title="Galápagos">Galápagos</a>, despite being included within Region 5,<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> is also under a special unit.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Ecuador" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Ecuador">Military of Ecuador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kfir_CE_front.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/Kfir_CE_front.JPG/220px-Kfir_CE_front.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/Kfir_CE_front.JPG/330px-Kfir_CE_front.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/Kfir_CE_front.JPG/440px-Kfir_CE_front.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1800" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_Air_Force" title="Ecuadorian Air Force">Ecuadorian Air Force</a> (FAE)</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Ecuador" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Ecuador">Ecuadorian Armed Forces</a> (Fuerzas Armadas de la Republica de Ecuador), consists of the <a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_Army" title="Ecuadorian Army">Army</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_Air_Force" title="Ecuadorian Air Force">Air Force</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_Navy" title="Ecuadorian Navy">Navy</a> and have the stated responsibility for the preservation of the integrity and national sovereignty of the national territory.
</p><p>Due to the continuous border disputes with Peru, finally settled in the early 2000s, and due to the ongoing problem with the Colombian guerrilla insurgency infiltrating Amazonian provinces, the Ecuadorian Armed Forces has gone through a series of changes. In 2009, the new administration at the Defense Ministry launched a deep restructuring within the forces, increasing spending budget to $1,691,776,803, an increase of 25%.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Military Academy General Eloy Alfaro (c. 1838) located in Quito is in charge of graduating army officers.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> The Ecuadorian Navy Academy (c. 1837), located in Salinas graduates navy officers.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> The Air Academy "Cosme Rennella (c. 1920), also located in Salinas, graduates air force officers.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Ecuador" title="Geography of Ecuador">Geography of Ecuador</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Rivers_of_Ecuador" class="mw-redirect" title="Rivers of Ecuador">Rivers of Ecuador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ecuador_Topography.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Ecuador_Topography.png/220px-Ecuador_Topography.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="245" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Ecuador_Topography.png/330px-Ecuador_Topography.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Ecuador_Topography.png/440px-Ecuador_Topography.png 2x" data-file-width="903" data-file-height="1005" /></a><figcaption>Ecuadorian topography</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Volc%C3%A1n_Chimborazo_desde_Riobamba.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Volc%C3%A1n_Chimborazo_desde_Riobamba.jpg/220px-Volc%C3%A1n_Chimborazo_desde_Riobamba.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="129" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Volc%C3%A1n_Chimborazo_desde_Riobamba.jpg/330px-Volc%C3%A1n_Chimborazo_desde_Riobamba.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Volc%C3%A1n_Chimborazo_desde_Riobamba.jpg/440px-Volc%C3%A1n_Chimborazo_desde_Riobamba.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3905" data-file-height="2281" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Chimborazo" title="Chimborazo">Chimborazo</a> volcano from <a href="/wiki/Riobamba" title="Riobamba">Riobamba</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Many_parrots_-Anangu,_Yasuni_National_Park,_Ecuador_-clay_lick-8.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Many_parrots_-Anangu%2C_Yasuni_National_Park%2C_Ecuador_-clay_lick-8.jpg/220px-Many_parrots_-Anangu%2C_Yasuni_National_Park%2C_Ecuador_-clay_lick-8.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Many_parrots_-Anangu%2C_Yasuni_National_Park%2C_Ecuador_-clay_lick-8.jpg/330px-Many_parrots_-Anangu%2C_Yasuni_National_Park%2C_Ecuador_-clay_lick-8.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Many_parrots_-Anangu%2C_Yasuni_National_Park%2C_Ecuador_-clay_lick-8.jpg/440px-Many_parrots_-Anangu%2C_Yasuni_National_Park%2C_Ecuador_-clay_lick-8.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3600" data-file-height="2400" /></a><figcaption>Birds in the <a href="/wiki/Yasuni_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="Yasuni National Park">Yasuni National Park</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Ecuador has a total area of 283,561&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (109,484&#160;sq&#160;mi), including the <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands" title="Galápagos Islands">Galápagos Islands</a>. Of this, 276,841&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (106,889&#160;sq&#160;mi) is land and 6,720&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (2,595&#160;sq&#160;mi) water.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> The Galápagos Islands are sometimes considered part of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-realm_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-realm-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-harv_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-harv-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> which would thus make Ecuador a <a href="/wiki/List_of_transcontinental_countries" title="List of transcontinental countries">transcontinental</a> country under certain definitions. Ecuador is bigger than Uruguay, Suriname, Guyana and French Guiana in South America.
</p><p>Ecuador lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/2nd_parallel_north" title="2nd parallel north">2°N</a> and <a href="/wiki/5th_parallel_south" title="5th parallel south">5°S</a>,
bounded on the west by the Pacific Ocean, and has 2,337&#160;km (1,452&#160;mi) of <a href="/wiki/Coastline" class="mw-redirect" title="Coastline">coastline</a>. It has 2,010&#160;km (1,250&#160;mi) of land boundaries, with <a href="/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia">Colombia</a> in the north (with a 590&#160;km (367&#160;mi) border) and Peru in the east and south (with a 1,420&#160;km (882&#160;mi) border). It is the westernmost country that lies on the equator.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country has four main geographic regions:
</p>
<ul><li><b>La Costa</b>, or "the coast": The coastal region consists of the provinces to the west of the Andean range&#160;– <a href="/wiki/Esmeraldas_Province" title="Esmeraldas Province">Esmeraldas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guayas_Province" title="Guayas Province">Guayas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Los_R%C3%ADos_Province" title="Los Ríos Province">Los Ríos</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manab%C3%AD_Province" title="Manabí Province">Manabí</a>, <a href="/wiki/El_Oro_Province" title="El Oro Province">El Oro</a>, Santo Domingo de los Tsachilas and <a href="/wiki/Santa_Elena_Province" title="Santa Elena Province">Santa Elena</a>. It is the country's most fertile and productive land, and is the seat of the large <a href="/wiki/Banana" title="Banana">banana</a> exportation plantations of the companies <a href="/wiki/Dole_Food_Company" class="mw-redirect" title="Dole Food Company">Dole</a> and <a href="/wiki/Chiquita" title="Chiquita">Chiquita</a>. This region is also where most of Ecuador's <a href="/wiki/Rice" title="Rice">rice</a> crop is grown. The truly coastal provinces have active fisheries. The largest coastal city is <a href="/wiki/Guayaquil" title="Guayaquil">Guayaquil</a>.</li>
<li><b>La Sierra</b>, or "the highlands": The sierra consists of the Andean and Interandean highland provinces&#160;– <a href="/wiki/Azuay_Province" title="Azuay Province">Azuay</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ca%C3%B1ar_Province" title="Cañar Province">Cañar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carchi_Province" title="Carchi Province">Carchi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chimborazo_Province" title="Chimborazo Province">Chimborazo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Imbabura_Province" title="Imbabura Province">Imbabura</a>, <a href="/wiki/Loja_Province" title="Loja Province">Loja</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pichincha_Province" title="Pichincha Province">Pichincha</a>, Bolívar,Cotopaxi and <a href="/wiki/Tungurahua_Province" title="Tungurahua Province">Tungurahua</a>. This land contains most of Ecuador's volcanoes and all of its snow-capped peaks. Agriculture is focused on the traditional crops of <a href="/wiki/Potato" title="Potato">potato</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maize" title="Maize">maize</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Quinoa" title="Quinoa">quinua</a> and the population is predominantly Amerindian <a href="/wiki/Quechua_people" title="Quechua people">Kichua</a>. The largest Sierran city is <a href="/wiki/Quito" title="Quito">Quito</a>.</li>
<li><b>La Amazonía</b>, also known as <i>El Oriente</i>, or "the east": The oriente consists of the <a href="/wiki/Amazon_rainforest" title="Amazon rainforest">Amazon jungle</a> provinces&#160;– <a href="/wiki/Morona_Santiago_Province" class="mw-redirect" title="Morona Santiago Province">Morona Santiago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Napo_Province" title="Napo Province">Napo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Orellana_Province" title="Orellana Province">Orellana</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pastaza_Province" title="Pastaza Province">Pastaza</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sucumb%C3%ADos_Province" title="Sucumbíos Province">Sucumbíos</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Zamora-Chinchipe_Province" title="Zamora-Chinchipe Province">Zamora-Chinchipe</a>. This region is primarily made up of the huge Amazon national parks and Amerindian untouchable zones, which are vast stretches of land set aside for the Amazon Amerindian tribes to continue living traditionally. It is also the area with the largest reserves of <a href="/wiki/Petroleum" title="Petroleum">petroleum</a> in Ecuador, and parts of the upper Amazon here have been extensively exploited by petroleum companies. The population is primarily mixed Amerindian <a href="/wiki/Shuar" title="Shuar">Shuar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Huaorani" class="mw-redirect" title="Huaorani">Huaorani</a> and <a href="/wiki/Quechua_people" title="Quechua people">Kichua</a>, although there are numerous tribes in the deep jungle which are little-contacted. The largest city in the Oriente is probably <a href="/wiki/Nueva_Loja" title="Nueva Loja">Lago Agrio</a> in Sucumbíos, although <a href="/wiki/Macas_(city)" title="Macas (city)">Macas</a> in Morona Santiago runs a close second.</li>
<li><b>La Región Insular</b> is the region comprising the <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands" title="Galápagos Islands">Galápagos Islands</a>, some 1,000 kilometers (620&#160;mi) west of the mainland in the Pacific Ocean.</li></ul>
<p>Ecuador's capital and largest city is Quito,<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> which is in the province of <a href="/wiki/Pichincha_Province,_Ecuador" class="mw-redirect" title="Pichincha Province, Ecuador">Pichincha</a> in the Sierra region. Its second largest city is Guayaquil,<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> in the <a href="/wiki/Guayas_Province" title="Guayas Province">Guayas Province</a>. <a href="/wiki/Cotopaxi" title="Cotopaxi">Cotopaxi</a>, just south of Quito, is one of the world's highest active volcanoes. The top of <a href="/wiki/Chimborazo_(volcano)" class="mw-redirect" title="Chimborazo (volcano)">Mount Chimborazo</a> (6,268 m, or 20,560&#160;ft, above sea level), Ecuador's tallest mountain, is the most distant point from the center of the Earth on the Earth's surface because of the <a href="/wiki/Ellipsoid" title="Ellipsoid">ellipsoid</a> shape of the planet.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
The Andes is the watershed divisor between the <a href="/wiki/Amazon_Basin" class="mw-redirect" title="Amazon Basin">Amazon</a> watershed, which runs to the east, and the Pacific, including the north–south rivers Mataje, Santiago, <a href="/wiki/Esmeraldas_River" title="Esmeraldas River">Esmeraldas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chone_River" title="Chone River">Chone</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guayas_River" title="Guayas River">Guayas</a>, Jubones, and Puyango-Tumbes.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Ecuador" title="Climate of Ecuador">Climate of Ecuador</a></div>
<p>There is great variety in the climate, largely determined by altitude. It is mild year-round in the mountain valleys, with a <a href="/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate" title="Humid subtropical climate">humid subtropical climate</a> in coastal areas and <a href="/wiki/Rainforest" title="Rainforest">rainforest</a> in lowlands. The Pacific coastal area has a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical climate</a> with a severe rainy season. The climate in the Andean highlands is <a href="/wiki/Temperateness" class="mw-redirect" title="Temperateness">temperate</a> and relatively dry, and the Amazon basin on the eastern side of the mountains shares the climate of other rainforest zones.
</p><p>Because of its location at the equator, Ecuador experiences little variation in daylight hours during the course of a year. Both sunrise and sunset occur each day at the two six o'clock hours.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_2-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country has seen its seven glaciers lose 54.4% of their surface in forty years. Research predicts their disappearance by 2100. The cause is climate change, which threatens both the fauna and flora and the population.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Biodiversity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Oso_andino_Porcon.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Oso_andino_Porcon.jpg/200px-Oso_andino_Porcon.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Oso_andino_Porcon.jpg/300px-Oso_andino_Porcon.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Oso_andino_Porcon.jpg/400px-Oso_andino_Porcon.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2851" data-file-height="2142" /></a><figcaption>Ecuador is one of the most <a href="/wiki/Megadiverse_countries" title="Megadiverse countries">megadiverse countries</a> in the world, it also has the most biodiversity per square kilometer of any nation, and is one of the highest <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemism</a> worldwide. In the image, the <a href="/wiki/Spectacled_bear" title="Spectacled bear">spectacled bear</a> of the Andes.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ecuador is one of seventeen <a href="/wiki/Megadiverse_countries" title="Megadiverse countries">megadiverse countries</a> in the world according to Conservation International,<sup id="cite_ref-Conservation_International_Site_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Conservation_International_Site-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> and it has the most biodiversity per square kilometer of any nation.<sup id="cite_ref-Plan_Nacional_del_Buen_Vivir_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Plan_Nacional_del_Buen_Vivir-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ecuador has 1,600 bird species (15% of the world's known bird species) in the continental area and 38 more <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic</a> in the Galápagos. In addition to more than 16,000 species of plants, the country has 106 endemic reptiles, 138 endemic amphibians, and 6,000 species of butterfly. The Galápagos Islands are well known as a region of distinct fauna, as the famous place of birth to Darwin's <a href="/wiki/Theory_of_Evolution" class="mw-redirect" title="Theory of Evolution">Theory of Evolution</a>, and as a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> World Heritage Site.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ecuador has the first constitution to recognize the <a href="/wiki/Rights_of_Nature" class="mw-redirect" title="Rights of Nature">rights of nature</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> The protection of the nation's biodiversity is an explicit national priority as stated in the National Plan of "Buen Vivir", or good living, Objective 4, "Guarantee the rights of nature", Policy 1: "Sustainably conserve and manage the natural heritage, including its land and marine biodiversity, which is considered a strategic sector".<sup id="cite_ref-Plan_Nacional_del_Buen_Vivir_98-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Plan_Nacional_del_Buen_Vivir-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ecuadorian_Amazon_rain_forest,_looking_toward_the_Andes.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Ecuadorian_Amazon_rain_forest%2C_looking_toward_the_Andes.jpg/220px-Ecuadorian_Amazon_rain_forest%2C_looking_toward_the_Andes.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Ecuadorian_Amazon_rain_forest%2C_looking_toward_the_Andes.jpg/330px-Ecuadorian_Amazon_rain_forest%2C_looking_toward_the_Andes.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Ecuadorian_Amazon_rain_forest%2C_looking_toward_the_Andes.jpg/440px-Ecuadorian_Amazon_rain_forest%2C_looking_toward_the_Andes.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2896" data-file-height="1936" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Amazon_rainforest" title="Amazon rainforest">Amazon rainforest</a> in Ecuador</figcaption></figure>
<p>As of the writing of the plan in 2008, 19% of Ecuador's land area was in a protected area; however, the plan also states that 32% of the land must be protected in order to truly preserve the nation's biodiversity.<sup id="cite_ref-Plan_Nacional_del_Buen_Vivir_98-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Plan_Nacional_del_Buen_Vivir-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Current <a href="/wiki/List_of_national_parks_in_Ecuador" title="List of national parks in Ecuador">protected areas</a> include 11 national parks, 10 wildlife refuges, 9 ecological reserves, and other areas.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> A program begun in 2008, Sociobosque, is preserving another 2.3% of total land area (6,295&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, or 629,500 ha) by paying private landowners or community landowners (such as Amerindian tribes) incentives to maintain their land as native ecosystems such as native forests or grasslands. Eligibility and subsidy rates for this program are determined based on the poverty in the region, the number of hectares that will be protected, and the type of ecosystem of the land to be protected, among other factors.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Ecuador had a 2018 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 7.66/10, ranking it 35th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite being on the UNESCO list, the Galápagos are endangered by a range of negative environmental effects, threatening the existence of this exotic <a href="/wiki/Ecosystem" title="Ecosystem">ecosystem</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, <a href="/wiki/Petroecuador" title="Petroecuador">oil exploitation of the Amazon rainforest</a> has led to the release of billions of gallons of untreated wastes, gas, and crude oil into the environment,<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> contaminating ecosystems and causing detrimental health effects to Amerindian peoples.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> One of the best known examples is the <a href="/wiki/Chevron_Corporation" title="Chevron Corporation">Texaco-Chevron</a> case.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> This American <a href="/wiki/Petroleum_in_the_United_States" title="Petroleum in the United States">oil company</a> operated in the Ecuadorian Amazon region between 1964 and 1992. During this period, Texaco drilled 339 wells in 15 petroleum fields and abandoned 627 toxic wastewater pits. It is now known that these highly polluting and now obsolete technologies were used as a way to reduce expenses.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2022 the supreme court of Ecuador decided that "under no circumstances can a project be carried out that generates excessive sacrifices to the collective rights of communities and nature." It also required the government to respect the opinion of Indigenous peoples about different industrial projects on their land.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Ecuador" title="Economy of Ecuador">Economy of Ecuador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ecuador.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ecuador.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ecuador.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ecuador.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ecuador.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ecuador.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ecuador.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>GDP per capita development of Ecuador</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ecuador has a <a href="/wiki/Developing_economy" class="mw-redirect" title="Developing economy">developing economy</a> that is highly dependent on commodities, namely petroleum and agricultural products. The country is classified as an upper-middle-income country. Ecuador's economy is the eighth largest in Latin America and experienced an average growth of 4.6% between 2000 and 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag failed verification of its source citation(s). (July 2019)">failed verification</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> From 2007 to 2012, Ecuador's GDP grew at an annual average of 4.3 percent, above the average for Latin America and the Caribbean, which was 3.5%, according to the United Nations' Economic Commission for Latin American and the Caribbean (ECLAC).<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Ecuador was able to maintain relatively superior growth during the <a href="/wiki/Financial_crisis_of_2007%E2%80%932008" class="mw-redirect" title="Financial crisis of 2007–2008">crisis</a>. In January 2009, the Central Bank of Ecuador (BCE) put the 2010 growth forecast at 6.88%.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, its GDP grew at 8% and ranked third highest in Latin America, behind Argentina (2nd) and Panama (1st).<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Between 1999 and 2007, GDP doubled, reaching $65,490 million according to BCE.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
The inflation rate until January 2008, was about 1.14%, the highest in the past year, according to the government.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The monthly unemployment rate remained at about 6 and 8 percent from December 2007 until September 2008; however, it went up to about 9 percent in October and dropped again in November 2008 to 8 percent.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Unemployment mean annual rate for 2009 in Ecuador was 8.5% because the global economic crisis continued to affect the Latin American economies. From this point, unemployment rates started a downward trend: 7.6% in 2010, 6.0% in 2011, and 4.8% in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The extreme poverty rate declined significantly between 1999 and 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> In 2001, it was estimated at 40% of the population, while by 2011 the figure dropped to 17.4% of the total population.<sup id="cite_ref-economy_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-economy-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> This is explained to an extent by emigration and the economic stability achieved after adopting the U.S. dollar as official means of transaction (before 2000, the Ecuadorian <i><a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_sucre" title="Ecuadorian sucre">sucre</a></i> was prone to rampant inflation). However, starting in 2008, with the bad economic performance of the nations where most Ecuadorian emigrants work, the reduction of poverty has been realized through social spending, mainly in education and health.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Obverse_of_the_series_2009_$100_Federal_Reserve_Note.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Obverse_of_the_series_2009_%24100_Federal_Reserve_Note.jpg/250px-Obverse_of_the_series_2009_%24100_Federal_Reserve_Note.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="105" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Obverse_of_the_series_2009_%24100_Federal_Reserve_Note.jpg/375px-Obverse_of_the_series_2009_%24100_Federal_Reserve_Note.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Obverse_of_the_series_2009_%24100_Federal_Reserve_Note.jpg/500px-Obverse_of_the_series_2009_%24100_Federal_Reserve_Note.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="505" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/United_States_dollar" title="United States dollar">United States dollar</a> is the common <a href="/wiki/Currency_of_Ecuador" title="Currency of Ecuador">currency circulation in Ecuador</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Oil accounts for 40% of exports and contributes to maintaining a positive trade balance.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Since the late 1960s, the exploitation of oil increased production, and proven reserves are estimated at 6.51 billion barrels as of 2011<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> In late 2021, Ecuador had to declare a <a href="/wiki/Force_majeure" title="Force majeure">Force majeure</a> for oil exports due to erosion near key pipelines (privately owned OCP pipeline and state-owned SOTE pipeline) in the Amazon.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> It lasted about three weeks, totalling just over $500 million economic losses, before their production returned to its normal level of 435,000 barrels per day (69,200&#160;m<sup>3</sup>/d) in early 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The overall trade balance for August 2012 was a surplus of almost $390 million for the first six months of 2012, a huge figure compared with that of 2007, which reached only $5.7 million; the surplus had risen by about $425 million compared to 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-economy_122-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-economy-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> The oil trade balance positive had revenues of $3.295 million in 2008, while non-oil was negative, amounting to $2.842 million. The trade balance with the United States, Chile, the European Union, Bolivia, Peru, Brazil, and Mexico is positive. The trade balance with Argentina, Colombia, and Asia is negative.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the agricultural sector, Ecuador is a major exporter of bananas (first place worldwide in export<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>), flowers, and the seventh largest producer of cocoa.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Ecuador also produces coffee, rice, potatoes, cassava (manioc, tapioca), plantains and sugarcane; cattle, sheep, pigs, beef, pork and dairy products; fish, and shrimp; and balsa wood.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> The country's vast resources include large amounts of timber across the country, like eucalyptus and mangroves.<sup id="cite_ref-Resources_Maps_World_Banks_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Resources_Maps_World_Banks-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> Pines and cedars are planted in the region of La Sierra and walnuts, rosemary, and balsa wood in the Guayas River Basin.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
The industry is concentrated mainly in Guayaquil, the largest industrial center, and in Quito, where in recent years the industry has grown considerably. This city is also the largest business center of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Industrial production is directed primarily to the domestic market.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Despite this, there is limited export of products produced or processed industrially.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> These include canned foods, liquor, jewelry, furniture, and more.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> A minor industrial activity is also concentrated in Cuenca.<sup id="cite_ref-Industrias_en_Cuenca_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Industrias_en_Cuenca-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> Incomes from tourism has been increasing during the last few years because of the Government showing the variety of climates and the biodiversity of Ecuador.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Wtcgye.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Wtcgye.jpg/180px-Wtcgye.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="127" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Wtcgye.jpg/270px-Wtcgye.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Wtcgye.jpg/360px-Wtcgye.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2892" data-file-height="2048" /></a><figcaption>World Trade Center headquarters in <a href="/wiki/Guayaquil" title="Guayaquil">Guayaquil</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Ecuador has negotiated bilateral treaties with other countries, besides belonging to the <a href="/wiki/Andean_Community_of_Nations" class="mw-redirect" title="Andean Community of Nations">Andean Community of Nations</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> and an associate member of <a href="/wiki/Mercosur" title="Mercosur">Mercosur</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> It also serves on the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a> (WTO), in addition to the <a href="/wiki/Inter-American_Development_Bank" title="Inter-American Development Bank">Inter-American Development Bank</a> (IDB), <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> (IMF), <a href="/wiki/CAF_%E2%80%93_Development_Bank_of_Latin_America_and_the_Caribbean" title="CAF – Development Bank of Latin America and the Caribbean">CAF – Development Bank of Latin America and the Caribbean</a> and other multilateral agencies.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> In April 2007, Ecuador paid off its debt to the IMF, thus ending an era of interventionism of the Agency in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
The public finance of Ecuador consists of the Central Bank of Ecuador (BCE), the National Development Bank (BNF), the State Bank.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sciences_and_research">Sciences and research</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Sciences and research">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:NEE-01-PEGASO.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/NEE-01-PEGASO.jpg/220px-NEE-01-PEGASO.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/NEE-01-PEGASO.jpg/330px-NEE-01-PEGASO.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/NEE-01-PEGASO.jpg/440px-NEE-01-PEGASO.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2736" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_Civilian_Space_Agency" title="Ecuadorian Civilian Space Agency">EXA</a>'s first satellite, <a href="/wiki/NEE-01_Pegasus" class="mw-redirect" title="NEE-01 Pegasus">NEE-01 Pegasus</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Ecuador was placed in 96th position of innovation in technology in a 2013 <a href="/wiki/World_Economic_Forum" title="World Economic Forum">World Economic Forum</a> study.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> Ecuador was ranked 91st in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021 up from 99th in 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> The most notable icons in Ecuadorian sciences are the mathematician and cartographer <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Vicente_Maldonado" title="Pedro Vicente Maldonado">Pedro Vicente Maldonado</a>, born in Riobamba in 1707, and the printer, independence precursor, and medical pioneer <a href="/wiki/Eugenio_Espejo" title="Eugenio Espejo">Eugenio Espejo</a>, born in 1747 in Quito. Among other notable Ecuadorian scientists and engineers are Lieutenant Jose Rodriguez Labandera,<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> a pioneer who built the first <a href="/wiki/Submarine" title="Submarine">submarine</a> in Latin America in 1837; <a href="https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinaldo_Espinosa_Aguilar" class="extiw" title="es:Reinaldo Espinosa Aguilar">Reinaldo Espinosa Aguilar</a>, a botanist and biologist of Andean flora; and <a href="https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Aurelio_Due%C3%B1as" class="extiw" title="es:José Aurelio Dueñas">José Aurelio Dueñas</a>, a chemist and inventor of a method of textile serigraphy.
</p><p>The major areas of scientific research in Ecuador have been in the medical fields, tropical and infectious diseases treatments, agricultural engineering, pharmaceutical research, and bioengineering. Being a small country and a consumer of foreign technology, Ecuador has favored research supported by entrepreneurship in information technology. The antivirus program <i>Checkprogram</i>, banking protection system <i>MdLock</i>, and Core Banking Software <i>Cobis</i> are products of Ecuadorian development.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Ecuador" title="Tourism in Ecuador">Tourism in Ecuador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Roof_deck_(Palacio_de_Pizarro),_Plaza_de_la_Independencia_(Quito),_pic.aa4.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Roof_deck_%28Palacio_de_Pizarro%29%2C_Plaza_de_la_Independencia_%28Quito%29%2C_pic.aa4.jpg/220px-Roof_deck_%28Palacio_de_Pizarro%29%2C_Plaza_de_la_Independencia_%28Quito%29%2C_pic.aa4.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Roof_deck_%28Palacio_de_Pizarro%29%2C_Plaza_de_la_Independencia_%28Quito%29%2C_pic.aa4.jpg/330px-Roof_deck_%28Palacio_de_Pizarro%29%2C_Plaza_de_la_Independencia_%28Quito%29%2C_pic.aa4.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Roof_deck_%28Palacio_de_Pizarro%29%2C_Plaza_de_la_Independencia_%28Quito%29%2C_pic.aa4.jpg/440px-Roof_deck_%28Palacio_de_Pizarro%29%2C_Plaza_de_la_Independencia_%28Quito%29%2C_pic.aa4.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4896" data-file-height="3672" /></a><figcaption>The historic center of <a href="/wiki/Quito" title="Quito">Quito</a> has one of the largest preserved historic centers in South America.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>Ecuador is a country with vast natural wealth. The diversity of its four regions has given rise to thousands of species of flora and fauna. It has approximately 1640 kinds of birds. The species of butterflies border 4,500, the reptiles 345, the amphibians 358, and the mammals 258, among others. Not in vain, Ecuador is considered one of the 17 countries where the planet's highest biodiversity is concentrated, being also the largest country with diversity per km2 in the world. Most of its fauna and flora lives in 26 protected areas by the state.
</p><p>The country has two cities with UNESCO World Heritage Sites: Quito and Cuenca, as well as two natural UNESCO World Heritage Sites: the Galapagos Islands and Sangay National Park in addition to one <a href="/wiki/World_Network_of_Biosphere_Reserves" title="World Network of Biosphere Reserves">World Biosphere Reserve</a>, such as the Cajas Massif. Culturally, the Toquilla straw hat and the culture of the Zapara indigenous people are recognized. The most popular sites for national and foreign tourists have different nuances due to the various tourist activities offered by the country.
</p><p>Among the main tourist destinations are:
</p>
<ul><li>Nature attractions: <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands" title="Galápagos Islands">Galápagos Islands</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yasuni_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="Yasuni National Park">Yasuni National Park</a>, <a href="/wiki/El_Cajas_National_Park" title="El Cajas National Park">El Cajas National Park</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sangay_National_Park" title="Sangay National Park">Sangay National Park</a>, <a href="/wiki/Podocarpus_National_Park" title="Podocarpus National Park">Podocarpus National Park</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vilcabamba,_Ecuador" title="Vilcabamba, Ecuador">Vilcabamba</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ba%C3%B1os_de_Agua_Santa" title="Baños de Agua Santa">Baños de Agua Santa</a>.</li>
<li>Cultural attractions: <a href="/wiki/Quito" title="Quito">Historic center of Quito</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ciudad_Mitad_del_Mundo" title="Ciudad Mitad del Mundo">Ciudad Mitad del Mundo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ingapirca" title="Ingapirca">Ingapirca</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cuenca,_Ecuador" title="Cuenca, Ecuador">Historic center of Cuenca</a>, <a href="/wiki/Latacunga" title="Latacunga">Latacunga</a> and its <a href="/wiki/Mama_Negra" title="Mama Negra">Mama Negra</a> festival.</li>
<li>Snowy mountains: <a href="/wiki/Antisana" title="Antisana">Antisana</a> volcano, <a href="/wiki/Cayambe_(volcano)" title="Cayambe (volcano)">Cayambe</a> volcano, <a href="/wiki/Chimborazo" title="Chimborazo">Chimborazo</a> volcano, <a href="/wiki/Cotopaxi" title="Cotopaxi">Cotopaxi</a> volcano, <a href="/wiki/Illiniza" title="Illiniza">Illinizas</a> volcanoes.</li>
<li>Beaches: <a href="/wiki/Atacames" title="Atacames">Atacames</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%ADa_de_Car%C3%A1quez" title="Bahía de Caráquez">Bahía de Caráquez</a>, Crucita, <a href="/wiki/Esmeraldas,_Ecuador" title="Esmeraldas, Ecuador">Esmeraldas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manta,_Ecuador" title="Manta, Ecuador">Manta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Monta%C3%B1ita" title="Montañita">Montañita</a>, <a href="/wiki/Playas,_Ecuador" title="Playas, Ecuador">Playas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Salinas,_Ecuador" title="Salinas, Ecuador">Salinas</a></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Quito_Trole_06_2011_1933.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Quito_Trole_06_2011_1933.jpg/220px-Quito_Trole_06_2011_1933.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="127" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Quito_Trole_06_2011_1933.jpg/330px-Quito_Trole_06_2011_1933.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Quito_Trole_06_2011_1933.jpg/440px-Quito_Trole_06_2011_1933.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1738" data-file-height="1000" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Trolleybuses_in_Quito" title="Trolleybuses in Quito">Trolebús</a> bus rapid transit system that runs through <a href="/wiki/Quito" title="Quito">Quito</a>. It is the principal BRT in Ecuador.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Railways_in_Ecuador.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Railways_in_Ecuador.svg/220px-Railways_in_Ecuador.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="299" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Railways_in_Ecuador.svg/330px-Railways_in_Ecuador.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Railways_in_Ecuador.svg/440px-Railways_in_Ecuador.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="588" data-file-height="800" /></a><figcaption>Railways in Ecuador <span class="nowrap">(<a href="https://meta.wikimedia.org/w/index.php?title=Interwiki_map/discontinued#Sharemap" class="extiw" title="sharemap:public/Railways in Ecuador">interactive map</a>)</span></figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Ecuador" title="Transport in Ecuador">Transport in Ecuador</a></div>
<p>The rehabilitation and reopening of the Ecuadorian railroad and use of it as a tourist attraction is one of the recent developments in transportation matters.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The roads of Ecuador in recent years have undergone important improvement. The major routes are Pan American (under enhancement from four to six lanes from Rumichaca to Ambato, the conclusion of 4 lanes on the entire stretch of Ambato and Riobamba and running via Riobamba to Loja). In the absence of the section between Loja and the border with Peru, there are the Route Espondilus and/or Ruta del Sol (oriented to travel along the Ecuadorian coastline) and the Amazon backbone (which crosses from north to south along the Ecuadorian Amazon, linking most and more major cities of it).
</p><p>Another major project is developing the road Manta – Tena, the highway Guayaquil – Salinas Highway Aloag Santo Domingo, Riobamba – Macas (which crosses Sangay National Park). Other new developments include the National Unity bridge complex in Guayaquil, the bridge over the Napo river in Francisco de Orellana, the Esmeraldas River Bridge in the city of the same name, and, perhaps the most remarkable of all, the Bahia – San Vincente Bridge, being the largest on the Latin American Pacific coast.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Cuenca,_Ecuador" title="Cuenca, Ecuador">Cuenca</a>'s tramway is the largest public transport system in the city and the first modern tramway in Ecuador. It was inaugurated on 8 March 2019. It has 20.4 kilometers (12.7&#160;mi) and 27 stations. It will transport 120,000 passagers daily. Its route starts in the south of Cuenca and ends in the north at the Parque Industrial neighbourhood.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Mariscal_Sucre_International_Airport" title="Mariscal Sucre International Airport">Mariscal Sucre International Airport</a> in Quito and the <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Joaqu%C3%ADn_de_Olmedo_International_Airport" title="José Joaquín de Olmedo International Airport">José Joaquín de Olmedo International Airport</a> in <a href="/wiki/Guayaquil" title="Guayaquil">Guayaquil</a> have experienced a high increase in demand and have required modernization. In the case of Guayaquil it involved a new air terminal, once considered the best in South America and the best in Latin America<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> and in Quito where an entire new airport has been built in Tababela and was inaugurated in February 2013, with Canadian assistance. However, the main road leading from Quito city center to the new airport will only be finished in late 2014, making current travelling from the airport to downtown Quito as long as two hours during rush hour.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> Quito's old city-center airport is being turned into parkland, with some light industrial use.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Ecuador" title="Demographics of Ecuador">Demographics of Ecuador</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Ecuadorian people">Ecuadorian people</a> and <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_in_Ecuador" title="Indigenous peoples in Ecuador">Indigenous peoples in Ecuador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ecuador_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Ecuador_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png/220px-Ecuador_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="177" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Ecuador_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png/330px-Ecuador_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Ecuador_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png/440px-Ecuador_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png 2x" data-file-width="2212" data-file-height="1777" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Population_pyramid" title="Population pyramid">Population pyramid</a> in 2020</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ecuador's population is ethnically diverse and the 2021 estimates put Ecuador's population at 17,797,737.<sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_2022_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> The largest ethnic group (as of 2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>) is the <i><a href="/wiki/Mestizo" title="Mestizo">Mestizos</a></i>, who are mixed race people of Amerindian and European descent, typically from Spanish colonists, in some cases this term can also include Amerindians that are culturally more Spanish influenced, and constitute about 71% of the population (although including the <a href="/wiki/Montubio" title="Montubio">Montubio</a>, a term used for coastal Mestizo population, brings this up to about 79%).
</p><p>The White Ecuadorians (<a href="/wiki/White_Latin_American" class="mw-redirect" title="White Latin American">White Latin American</a>) are a minority accounting for 6.1% of the population of Ecuador and can be found throughout all of Ecuador, primarily around the urban areas. Even though Ecuador's white population during its colonial era were mainly descendants from Spain, today Ecuador's white population is a result of a mixture of European immigrants, predominantly from Spain with people from Italy, Germany, France, and Switzerland who have settled in the early 20th century. In addition, there is a small European Jewish (<a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_Jews" class="mw-redirect" title="Ecuadorian Jews">Ecuadorian Jews</a>) population, which is based mainly in Quito and to a lesser extent in Guayaquil.<sup id="cite_ref-ethn_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ethn-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ecuador also has a small population of Asian origins, mainly those from West Asia, like the economically well off descendants of Lebanese and Palestinian immigrants, who are either Christian or Muslim (see <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Ecuador" title="Islam in Ecuador">Islam in Ecuador</a>), and an East Asian community mainly consisting of those of Japanese and Chinese descent, whose ancestors arrived as miners, farmhands and fishermen in the late 19th century.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_2-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas" title="Indigenous peoples of the Americas">Amerindians</a> account for 7% of the current population. The mostly rural Montubio population of the coastal provinces of Ecuador, who might be classified as <a href="/wiki/Pardo" title="Pardo">Pardo</a> account for 7.4% of the population.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Afro-Ecuadorian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Afro-Ecuadorian people">Afro-Ecuadorians</a> are a minority population (7%) in Ecuador, that includes the <a href="/wiki/Mulatto" title="Mulatto">Mulattos</a> and <i><a href="/wiki/Zambo" title="Zambo">zambos</a></i>, and are largely based in the Esmeraldas province and to a lesser degree in the predominantly Mestizo provinces of Coastal Ecuador – Guayas and Manabi. In the Highland Andes where a predominantly Mestizo, white and Amerindian population exist, the African presence is almost non-existent except for a small community in the province of Imbabura called Chota Valley. 5,000 <a href="/wiki/Romani_people" title="Romani people">Romani people</a> live in Ecuador.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Language">Language</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Language">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Ecuador" title="Languages of Ecuador">Languages of Ecuador</a></div>
<div class="barbox tleft" style="overflow-x: auto;">

<p>Spanish is the official language in Ecuador. It is spoken as a first (93%) or second language (6%) by the vast majority of its population. In 1991 Northern <a href="/wiki/Kichwa" class="mw-redirect" title="Kichwa">Kichwa</a> (Quechua) and other pre-colonial American languages were spoken by 2,500,000 but in the meantime its use has dropped significantly. Ethnologues estimate that the country has about 24 living indigenous languages. Except of Quechua the other indigenous languages are spoken by very few people, the largest of these languages spoken by 0,5 percent of the population. Among the 24 are <a href="/wiki/Awa_Pit_language" title="Awa Pit language">Awapit</a> (spoken by the Awá), <a href="/wiki/A%27ingae" class="mw-redirect" title="A&#39;ingae">A'ingae</a> (spoken by the Cofan), <a href="/wiki/Shuar_language" title="Shuar language">Shuar Chicham</a> (spoken by the Shuar), <a href="/wiki/Achuar-Shiwiar" class="mw-redirect" title="Achuar-Shiwiar">Achuar-Shiwiar</a> (spoken by the Achuar and the Shiwiar), <a href="/wiki/Cha%27palaachi" class="mw-redirect" title="Cha&#39;palaachi">Cha'palaachi</a> (spoken by the Chachi), <a href="/wiki/Tsafiki_language" title="Tsafiki language">Tsa'fiki</a> (spoken by the Tsáchila), <a href="/wiki/Pai_Coca_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Pai Coca language">Paicoca</a> (spoken by the Siona and Secoya), and <a href="/wiki/Waorani_language" title="Waorani language">Wao Tededeo</a> (spoken by the Waorani). Use of these Amerindian languages is gradually diminishing. 
</p><p>Most Ecuadorians speak Spanish as their first language, with its ubiquity permeating and dominating most of the country. Despite its small size the country has a marked diversity in Spanish accents that vary widely among regions. <a href="/wiki/Ecuadorian_Spanish" title="Ecuadorian Spanish">Ecuadorian Spanish</a> idiosyncrasies reflect the ethnic and racial populations that originated and settled the distinct areas of the country. 
</p><p>The three main regional variants are:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Equatorial_Pacific_Spanish_or_Equatorial_Coastal_Spanish" class="mw-redirect" title="Equatorial Pacific Spanish or Equatorial Coastal Spanish">Equatorial Pacific Spanish or Equatorial Coastal Spanish</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Andean_Spanish" title="Andean Spanish">Andean Spanish</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Amazonic_Spanish" title="Amazonic Spanish">Amazonic Spanish</a></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ecuador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Ecuador" title="Religion in Ecuador">Religion in Ecuador</a></div>
<div class="barbox tleft" style="overflow-x: auto;">
<div style="border:1px solid silver; font-size:88%; padding:0.4em; width:auto; background: white;">
